3L.3 · Sprung truck development19 October 2026 UTC
Moapa Valley, Nevada
ENGINEERING REFERENCE · CALCULATIONS / RESEARCH / REPORTSProject overviewDrawings & fabrication →
Preliminary · Engineering review required · Not for construction
PULSIPHER FARMS / MOAPA VALLEY CORN MAZE
A real railway.
A coordinated review.
A diesel-powered Christmas passenger train with a steam-era appearance, enclosed coaches and a dedicated accessible car.
This technical edition is prepared for Ralph, prospective suppliers, engineers and fabricators. Use the drawings and design basis to identify a buildable solution, its interfaces and the evidence still needed.
Inspect connection details ↗Find your scopePreferred regular coach · illustrative space model

Candidate guest places3L.3
Sprung truck development
CURRENT FABRICATION DEVELOPMENT
Developing the complete sprung truck.
The complete sprung truck remains in development. Independent drawbar and locomotive-drive studies now add actual part geometry and traceable matching calculations. Neither system is released for fabrication.
Review current truck development →Inspect the drawbar part study →Review new locomotive matching →See the custom fabrication scope →
THE NEXT DECISION
Turn space studies into engineered interfaces.
Confirm the locomotive and running gear, a complete brake architecture, accessible boarding and evacuation, and the wheel–rail relationship before fixing fabrication dimensions.See the engineering hold points →
HOW TO USE THIS EDITION
Review by trade, then flag the exceptions.
Read the design basis and relevant drawing sheets.
Identify assumptions, deviations and interfaces supplied by others.
Return documented ratings, scope, engineering responsibility and a proposal.Download response template ↓
The CAD files describe preliminary space envelopes. File integrity and static plan checks do not establish structural strength, approved occupancy, stability, braking or code compliance.
3S.1 · CONTROLLED SOURCE / DRAWING READINESS · 19 OCTOBER 2026
What is actually drawn—and what is still missing?
This is a traceable index of17current packages, not another schematic or a new fabrication drawing. It links the exact existing source, model and drawing evidence to the physical connections still needed. All17packages remain unreleased; the whole desktop program is not complete.
Start here, Ralph and fabricators
A rotatable model may show a space reservation, a trial physical part or a display-only mechanism. None automatically supplies materials, fits, joints or an accepted load path. The list below says which kind you are opening. Sources are preserved—not silently replaced.
Specific audit: J1a has110 nominal members; the register expressly omits the separate I1connection details, bogie mounts, couplings and brakes. I1has six local assemblies and160mesh objects, including weld representations—not160released fabricated pieces. No completion percentage is inferred.
Existing sources use explicit inches/mm as stated in each file. Exact source hashes and archive-member hashes are in the index. A hash confirms identity, not correctness, approval or physical fit. Linked archive members are identified so reviewers do not have to guess which STEP/DXF/register belongs to a study.
Consolidated closure registerEditable package / source indexEditable release / evidence register

PK-01 · trial physical member geometry
Regular-coach underframe J1a
Actually present: 110 nominal HSS member shells: 16 CM6 and 52 S32 substitutions plus42 retained members. Archive register contains member IDs, orientation, dimensions, bounds, material candidates and connections. Catalog-member subtotal1816.347kg is not coach tare.
Not build-ready: End planes are not saw lengths. Register explicitly excludes I-BA angles, I-BT boxes, I-SH1 hardpoints, weld solids, bogie mounts, couplings and brakes. No released joints, WPS, local-wall/fatigue acceptance or complete assembled frame.
Responsible disciplines: Vehicle structural engineer + qualified fabricator/welding and inspection specialists. Named acceptance professional: not assigned in this record.
Next useful detail: Reconcile every connection ID to one actual detail and drawing; integrate accepted supports and loads before machining/cutting release.
Release dependencies: SG-LOAD, SG-TRUCK, SG-JOINT, SG-QA. No completed fabrication drawing, fabrication release or accepted physical test is asserted for this package.Open existing assembly / review pageExact source evidence and archive members

PK-02 · trial physical local assemblies
Local frame connections I1
Actually present: Six local assembly studies and160 mesh objects include receivers, branches and weld-volume representations. Sections, angles, tapered boxes, seat hardpoints and floor clips are inspectable in assembled/exploded views.
Not build-ready: 160 mesh objects are not160 purchasable or fabricated parts. These details are NOT installed in the110-member J1a model. Represented weld volumes are not accepted weld sizes/procedures or demonstrated capacity.
Responsible disciplines: Vehicle structural engineer + welding engineer + fabricator. Named acceptance professional: not assigned in this record.
Next useful detail: Complete one load-path connection after its receiving members, stiffness, design loads, fit-up and inspection basis are accepted.
Release dependencies: SG-JOINT, SG-FLOOR, SG-QA. No completed fabrication drawing, fabrication release or accepted physical test is asserted for this package.Open existing assembly / review pageExact source evidence and archive members

PK-03 · layout and ground-level mockup geometry
Accessible coach structure and seat/boarding layout
Actually present: Separate two-wheelchair-bay layout, shifted aisle/end-door datum and companion seating studies. Stage2 accessible viewer has31display objects and an earlier28in floor, not a coordinated32in load-state vehicle.3E mockup provides11fixed places in8seat assemblies plus2wheelchair bays; actual-inch DXF and canonical coordinates exist. Observation/device/chair forms are blank records.
Not build-ready: Do not copy regular-coach seat feet/support schedule into this car. No accepted wheelchair/lift point loads, independent accessible frame/joint schedule or signed occupied circulation/evacuation result. Sparse DXF labels require the coordinate table. Different source floor datums must not be interpreted as one accepted geometry.
Responsible disciplines: Vehicle structural/accessibility designers + responsible authority. Named acceptance professional: not assigned in this record.
Next useful detail: Use existing full-size mockup protocol; map actual accepted seat, wheelchair, boarding and evacuation loads to a separate support schedule. Do not issue a duplicate mockup.
Release dependencies: SG-ACCESS, SG-LOAD, SG-TRUCK, SG-JOINT. No completed fabrication drawing, fabrication release or accepted physical test is asserted for this package.Open existing assembly / review pageExact source evidence and archive members

PK-04 · display-only assembly plus separate load/motion studies
Complete two-axle coach truck
Actually present: G1 visualizes truck yaw. Later3L.3-S0/S1 analyze body datum, trial coil loads, bearing candidates, axle sensitivity and guide/brake motion interfaces.
Not build-ready: G1 596mm wheels,1165mm wheelbase,2in axle and displayed hardware are not selected sizes. No complete physical axlebox, guide, spring/damper, stops/retention, frame, pivot/sidebearer or brake assembly has been defined.
Responsible disciplines: Rail vehicle/running-gear and dynamics engineers + bearing/brake/spring suppliers. Named acceptance professional: not assigned in this record.
Next useful detail: Close wheel/bearing/axlebox package and masses, then compatible guide articulation, brake reaction and twist/roll response; actual spring/damper envelopes precede mount sizes.
Release dependencies: SG-WHEEL, SG-BEARING, SG-TRUCK, SG-D1. No completed fabrication drawing, fabrication release or accepted physical test is asserted for this package.Open existing assembly / review pageExact source evidence and archive members

PK-05 · manufacturing requirements and bounded calculations
Rail wheels, rotating axles and removable drum hubs
Actually present: Wheel manufacturing traveler and dimension matrix identify missing evidence; S1 axle/bearing sensitivities are preserved. D1 service concept keeps wheel and bearing installed while custom outboard hub/drum are removed together.
Not build-ready: No selected rail profile/back-to-back/wear limits, wheel-seat fit, axle fatigue/retention detail or hub joint. A semi-trailer stationary spindle arrangement cannot be copied onto a rotating rail axle.
Responsible disciplines: Wheelset/rail contact engineer + specialist wheel/axle manufacturer and NDT inspector. Named acceptance professional: not assigned in this record.
Next useful detail: Complete exact wheel/rail and bearing arrangements before selecting journal, wheel-seat, hub-joint and machining fits; never derive dimensions from screenshots.
Release dependencies: SG-WHEEL, SG-BEARING, SG-D1, SG-QA. No completed fabrication drawing, fabrication release or accepted physical test is asserted for this package.Open existing assembly / review pageExact source evidence and archive members

PK-06 · documented family match; schematic mounting/service studies
D1 installed coach brakes and custom supports
Actually present: Dexter12.25×4 PQ S-cam with documentedType20/24 combination chamber is preferred coach prototype family:2brakes/axle,8/coach,32coach chambers. OEM drum-face orientation retained.
Not build-ready: L2-M01/M02/M03 are schematics, not fabrication drawings. Exact drum/pilot/holes, spider/cam/chamber envelopes, maximum torque curves, reverse modulation, custom hub/carrier/reaction geometry remain held. Locomotive package is separate.
Responsible disciplines: Brake/running-gear engineers + Dexter/application evidence + qualified fabricator. Named acceptance professional: not assigned in this record.
Next useful detail: Use controlled delivered D1 interfaces and full service/spring/fault torque with axlebox/guide motions; do not add an independently rigid reaction that locks suspension.
Release dependencies: SG-D1, SG-TRUCK, SG-PNEUMATIC, SG-QA. No completed fabrication drawing, fabrication release or accepted physical test is asserted for this package.Open existing assembly / review pageExact source evidence and archive members

PK-07 · functional architecture and bounded demand budget
Air trainlines, control and stored energy
Actually present: 3K contains trainline/state/failure architecture and test planning.3L.2 updates catalog-volume interfaces to32coach chambers; locomotive actuators and complete installed lines/valves remain separate.
Not build-ready: Functional topology is not a final port/plumbing or safety-controller implementation. Catalog volume sums do not validate charging, separation response, trapped pressure, brake balance, emergency timing or holding.
Responsible disciplines: Rail brake/control engineer + pneumatic application specialists. Named acceptance professional: not assigned in this record.
Next useful detail: Freeze actual locomotive+coach valves/chambers, lengths/volumes and power allocation; prepare instrumented full-consist failure tests with accepted limits before operation.
Release dependencies: SG-PNEUMATIC, SG-D1, SG-POWER, SG-TEST. No completed fabrication drawing, fabrication release or accepted physical test is asserted for this package.Open existing assembly / review pageExact source evidence and archive members

PK-08 · trial nominal physical part; incomplete assembly
Drawbar DB-01 and frame coupling joints
Actually present: DB-01 has editable nominal solid/profile and bounded train/part force cases. SKF GE60 ES-2RS is a catalog bearing comparison, not a selected joint.
Not build-ready: No released eye fit, retainers, pins/clevises, draft gear, frame attachment, secondary restraint or full-motion guards. Nominal DXF is not an NC cutting file; bearing catalog ratings are not coupling allowable loads.
Responsible disciplines: Vehicle structural/dynamics engineer + joint/bearing specialist. Named acceptance professional: not assigned in this record.
Next useful detail: Close complete pin/clevis/bearing/retention and frame load path under tension, compression, slack, fatigue and accepted articulation; then revise part and actual drawings.

PK-09 · component matching and alternative studies
Locomotive frame, powered running gear and propulsion
Actually present: Conditional hydrostatic lead: Kubota V3307-CR-TI-E5, Danfoss H1P045/H1B110 matching studies. Single-motor mechanically supported distribution is comparison lead; electric and mechanical routes retained.
Not build-ready: Not exact order codes or a construction-ready drive. No accepted continuous installed engine duty, complete powered chassis, supported reduction, adapter, mounts, cooling/exhaust, tank endurance or operator visibility envelope.
Responsible disciplines: Drivetrain/running-gear engineers + engine/hydraulic application specialists. Named acceptance professional: not assigned in this record.
Next useful detail: Obtain exact shaft/housing/continuous maps and vehicle arrangement; coordinate motor/reduction/axle relative motion and exhaust/cooling with occupied cars before physical adapter/frame detail.
Release dependencies: SG-DRIVE, SG-LOAD, SG-WHEEL, SG-JOINT, SG-TEST. No completed fabrication drawing, fabrication release or accepted physical test is asserted for this package.Open existing assembly / review pageExact source evidence and archive members

PK-10 · trial physical local parts with recorded interference problem
Floor panels, seat hardpoints and seams
Actually present: O1 reproduces actual I-SH1 sole/opening stack: nominal zero vertical isolation is not a working clearance. Regular coach has64 seat-foot penetrations;21panel thermal sensitivities are not joint qualification.
Not build-ready: No accepted stack with positive tolerance/load/thermal clearance, panel inserts/edge closeouts, fastener grip/locking, movement topology or moisture-qualified joint. Accessible car requires its own mapping.
Responsible disciplines: Vehicle structural/materials engineer + seat/panel/adhesive suppliers. Named acceptance professional: not assigned in this record.
Next useful detail: Resolve actual coupled hardpoint/panel stack and load bypass, then one exact production-material seat/panel coupon with defined proof/cyclic/thermal/wet acceptance.
Release dependencies: SG-FLOOR, SG-ACCESS, SG-JOINT, SG-TEST. No completed fabrication drawing, fabrication release or accepted physical test is asserted for this package.Open existing assembly / review pageExact source evidence and archive members

EV-051 · topology-and-operating-decision.json
One closure register:19open evidence gates
These are accountable disciplines, not invented appointments. No supplier return, signed approval, physical test or acceptance value has been fabricated. A gate closes only against actual accepted evidence and a controlled revision. Interacting designs require iteration; prerequisite links are a release-order aid, not a promise that design can proceed in one pass.
 | Gate · open
 | Responsible disciplines
 | Exact closure evidence
 | Current affected packages
 | SG-LOAD
Accepted duty, mass/CG and governing load basis
 | Vehicle structural/dynamics engineers
 | Controlled normal/fault/maintenance/transport load cases and fatigue spectrum; actual individual mass/CG allocation and wheel loads, including locomotive. Upper mass calculations are not measured tare or ratings.
Prerequisite closure: accepted project basis; no preceding gate assigned.
 | PK-01, PK-03, PK-08, PK-09
 | SG-WHEEL
Coordinated wheel/rail contact and gauge
 | Rail vehicle + track engineers, wheel manufacturer
 | Controlled new/worn wheel and rail profiles, back-to-back/gauge/reference definitions, flange/guard/frog clearances, minimum curve/transition and contact/stability assessment; accepted material/manufacturing basis.
Prerequisite closure: SG-LOAD.
 | PK-04, PK-05, PK-09, PK-15
 | SG-BEARING
Bearing/rotating axle/axlebox package
 | Wheelset engineer + bearing application specialist
 | Exact bearing designation/arrangement, life/load/deflection, fits/shoulders/radii/seals/setting/retention and controlled source CAD; accepted axle strength/fatigue and wheel-seat assembly/inspection procedure.
Prerequisite closure: SG-LOAD, SG-WHEEL.
 | PK-04, PK-05
 | SG-D1
Delivered D1 interfaces and all applied torque
 | Brake/running-gear engineers + actual OEM evidence
 | Exact assembled drum hub pilot/bolt/mating-face/service envelope; spider/cam/chamber geometry, service/spring force-stroke/torque including reverse/low-pressure/fault behavior; complete hub retention and stationary carrier interfaces.
Prerequisite closure: SG-BEARING.
 | PK-04, PK-05, PK-06, PK-07
 | SG-TRUCK
Suspension, guides, brake reaction and body support
 | Running-gear/dynamics/structural engineers
 | Whole-truck sprung/unsprung mass/CG; compatible guide tilt/travel and brake reaction; spring/damper curves and working envelopes; stops/retention, pivot/sidebearer law, empty/loaded/bump/roll/twist wheel-load/stability; exact physical joints.
Prerequisite closure: SG-LOAD, SG-BEARING, SG-D1.
 | PK-01, PK-03, PK-04, PK-06, PK-14
 | SG-COUPLING
Complete articulated coupling and load path

Prerequisite closure: SG-QA, SG-OPS, SG-COUPLING, SG-DRIVE.
 | PK-07, PK-08, PK-09, PK-10, PK-12, PK-13, PK-14, PK-16, PK-17
What a completed drawing must establish
Recognizable full assembly and cutaway/context; numbered physical parts matching its BOM; coordinated plan/elevation/section/details from the same geometry; actual mating faces, fasteners/retention, travel/service access; supported material, datum, dimensions/fits/tolerances, weld/joint specification and inspection points. Practical reader check: a builder can identify each part, each connection and what can be made without guessing. Drawing completeness and professional fabrication release are separate decisions.
This index checks file identity, source linkage, package/gate coverage and current configuration consistency—not practical drawing completeness. No new assembly is called buildable. Browser interaction remains unverified because managed preview could not start; the index is static HTML using existing Site navigation.
Next3S.2: integrate fabrication/inspection travelers and assembly/installation/commissioning sequence using existing blank forms. Then continue supportable operations/emergency/maintenance/acceptance procedures. Do not repeat cosmetic HOLD sheets or call this completed3S,3L or a finished desktop package.

3S.4 · INTEGRATED HAZARD / CONTROL / VERIFICATION CROSSWALK · 19 OCTOBER 2026
Follow each failure mechanism through the whole train.
25specific mechanisms connect the17current assembly packages to existing commissioning/traveler records and19open release gates. These are proposed design responses to prove—not installed controls, accepted risks or a complete formal safety case. Severity, likelihood and residual risk remain unassessed; no invented risk score is used to close a design.
Next governing calculation: track twist can redistribute individual wheel loads despite0%mean grade. Existing S0 is heave-only; S1checks shafts, guide tilt and bearings. Neither solves four-wheel contact loads. We will develop that equilibrium calculation before claiming spring/guide mounts are sized. Spring-seat spacing is not nominal gauge, and corner spring force is not automatically wheel reaction.
Editable hazard / evidence registerRemaining-work auditCurrent physical truck scope
Open mechanisms and evidence to obtain
The final column links exact existing records to REVIEW or develop—not permission to run a test. Scoped component tests need their own accepted fixture/protocol/limits; complete-train release requires the coordinated accepted evidence. Source warnings and hashes do not prove physical mitigation.
 | Mechanism / packages
 | How it can fail
 | Response to define and prove
 | Verification / release subjects
 | HZ-01 · Track twist unloads an individual wheel
PK-01, PK-04, PK-05, PK-15
 | Four rail contact points are not coplanar. Rigid truck/guide/body constraints redistribute spring and wheel loads; a light wheel can lose guidance/braking reserve even with a0%mean grade.
 | Solve actual wheel/axlebox spring lever arms, truck flexibility/equalization, body support law and new/worn/empty/loaded wheel loads. Spring force is not automatically wheel reaction. Accept combined twist/curve/lateral/dynamic evidence.
 | E-E01, E-E07, E-E08, J-J-T09, J-J-T11, J-J-T12
SG-WHEEL, SG-TRUCK, SG-TRACK, SG-TEST
 | HZ-02 · Guide tilt and brake reaction bind suspension
PK-04, PK-06
 | Wheelset tilt in a long close-fitting guide, or a second incompatible rigid brake-reaction link, can lock intended motion and distort bearings/carrier while transferring unintended forces into frame.
 | Define permitted translations/rotations and compliant joints, bearing/guide alignment and actual maximum service/spring/fault reactions; verify combined motion under load, not a projected clearance alone.
 | E-E01, J-J-T09, J-J-T12
SG-BEARING, SG-D1, SG-TRUCK, SG-JOINT
 | HZ-03 · Wheel/axle/bearing or removable-hub retention fails
PK-04, PK-05, PK-06
 | Unaccepted press fits, rotating-axle bearing setting, axle fatigue or removable drum-hub joint can loosen/fracture and lose wheel guidance or brake torque. Semi-trailer stationary-spindle geometry is not the same load path.
 | Complete controlled wheel/rail profile, wheel-seat/journal/hub fit and fatigue/retention arrangement, materials/heat/NDT and exact bearing support; preserve bearing and pressed wheel during hub/drum service without compromising axle retention.
 | E-E01, J-J-T10, J-J-T12
SG-WHEEL, SG-BEARING, SG-D1, SG-QA
 | HZ-04 · Body rolls, lifts or separates from truck
PK-01, PK-03, PK-04, PK-14
 | Wide occupied body, mass/CG changes, lateral acceleration/wind, sidebearer contact or failed pivot retention can unload wheels, strike structure or separate body/truck.
 | Accept whole-coach mass/CG and combined roll/curve/wind/twist loads, actual center/pivot/sidebearer geometry and retention, clearance and structural path. Neither nominal gauge nor free spring travel proves stability.
 | E-E01, E-E08, J-J-T09, J-J-T11, J-J-T12
SG-LOAD, SG-TRUCK, SG-JOINT, SG-TEST
 | HZ-05 · Spring/damper/stop seat overload or loss of retention
PK-04, PK-06
 | Empty/loaded travel, spring solid height/unseating, damper end load or stop impact can fail a seat/guide/frame or damage the aligned brake assembly.
 | Choose actual force/travel/envelope/damping/stop energy and load paths after masses/motions are established; qualify upper/lower seat joints and drop-out retention. Trial8coils and heave frequencies are not selected hardware.
 | E-E01, J-J-T09, J-J-T10, J-J-T12
SG-LOAD, SG-TRUCK, SG-JOINT
 | HZ-06 · D1 maximum applied torque overwhelms custom support
PK-04, PK-05, PK-06
 | Sizing carrier/hub/cam/chamber support only for target deceleration ignores higher service/spring/fault torque and reversing sign; support or retention may fail before a wheel can safely transmit it.
 | Obtain exact delivered D1 curves/envelopes and matched geometry; design hub/carrier/axlebox/guide/frame joints for accepted governing combinations, heat/fatigue and full movement. Keep OEM drum mounting-face orientation.
 | E-E03, E-E06, E-E08, J-J-T12, K-K-ST18
SG-D1, SG-TRUCK, SG-JOINT, SG-TEST
 | HZ-07 · Brake force is excessive or uncontrollable on rail
PK-05, PK-06, PK-07
 | D1 family match does not establish low-torque modulation, reverse behavior or empty/loaded adhesion balance. Service/spring compounding can slide wheels or produce high jerk/asymmetry.
 | Map pressure/stroke/output torque and spring transfer, wear/temperature and both directions; accept load control, anti-compounding and wheel-slide/fault response on actual wheel/rail/consist.
 | E-E03, E-E06, E-E07, E-E08, E-E09, K-K-ST02, K-K-ST04, K-K-ST14
SG-D1, SG-PNEUMATIC, SG-WHEEL, SG-TEST
 | HZ-08 · Air trainline loss does not stop both separated portions
PK-07, PK-08, PK-17
 | Wrong/self-sealed connector, closed cock, masked BP leak, depleted reserve or electrical-jumper persistence can leave a vehicle/portion without commanded braking. MR-held spring release does not necessarily vent with BP loss.
 | Complete actual BP/MR/valve/connector/permit design and usable reserve:32coach chambers plus separate locomotive/lines/valves. Prove every joint/both portions and isolation/feed/vent faults, not pressure at the locomotive only.
 | E-E05, E-E08, K-K-ST05, K-K-ST06, K-K-ST07, K-K-ST10, K-K-ST17, K-K-ST20
SG-PNEUMATIC, SG-COUPLING, SG-POWER, SG-TEST
 | HZ-09 · Normal pressure indication hides mechanical brake failure
PK-06, PK-07, PK-17
 | Relay/pilot pressure or software state can appear correct while cam/slack/lining/drum/hub/carrier is failed, dragging or not producing wheel torque; service and spring sections share mechanical elements.
 | Coordinate delivered-pressure, mechanical stroke/condition/output/release and heat/slide evidence; detect disagreement and prohibit dispatch. Do not credit a second chamber section as mechanical redundancy.
 | E-E04, E-E08, K-K-ST11, K-K-ST12, K-K-ST18, K-K-ST19
SG-D1, SG-PNEUMATIC, SG-OPS, SG-TEST
 | HZ-10 · Parking or maintenance release permits rollaway
PK-06, PK-07, PK-14, PK-17
 | Unproved spring holding, caging, air/power changes, uncoupling or the service-to-spring handover can remove restraint while people board or service the train.0%target is not an independent restraint.
 | Accept parked/boarding/storage/rescue holding and independent task-specific restraint, actual force handover and restore/retest. No generic caging or vent-all-air procedure; disabled brakes remain out of passenger service.
 | E-E06, K-K-ST04, K-K-ST08, K-K-ST15, K-K-ST19
SG-D1, SG-PNEUMATIC, SG-ACCESS, SG-OPS
 | HZ-11 · Coupling or frame attachment fails under compression/slack
PK-01, PK-02, PK-08, PK-09
 | Braking imbalance, reversing, draft slack/impact or articulation can overload eye/pin/retainer/clevis/draft/frame joints. Catalog bearing force or nominal bar stress does not rate the complete coupling.
 | Complete accepted force/duty/slack/fatigue and articulation, pin/eye fits and retention, draft gear, secondary restraint, guards and main-frame load path; qualify actual joints and protected service access.
 | E-E05, E-E08, J-J-T10, J-J-T12, K-K-ST07, K-K-ST16
SG-COUPLING, SG-LOAD, SG-JOINT, SG-TEST
 | HZ-12 · Nominal frame/detail geometry is cut as released steel
PK-01, PK-02, PK-10, PK-16
 | J1a member envelopes omit local I1connections/support/brakes/couplings. End-plane lengths, sharp-corner HSS and weld representations can be mistaken for shop lengths, delivered profiles or qualified welds.
 | Integrate actual receiving members/joints, fit-up/end preparation/distortion, supported material/WPS/fatigue/inspection and access. Link every part/connection ID and actual drawing revision; source identity is not adequacy.
 | J-J-T01, J-J-T06, J-J-T07, J-J-T10, J-J-T11, TR-J-TR01, TR-J-TR05, TR-J-TR07, TR-J-TR08
SG-LOAD, SG-JOINT, SG-QA
 | HZ-13 · Seat load bypass bears on or damages floor
PK-01, PK-02, PK-03, PK-10
 | O1 finds nominal zero vertical isolation between I-SH1 sole and floor stack. Tolerances, floor/seat deflection and thermal movement can transfer load into unqualified panel/insert/seal or loosen attachment.
 | Revise the coupled sole/cap/spacer/opening/fastener/seal stack with positive accepted clearance under governing loads/temperature. Qualify exact seat/panel coupon;64regular feet are not the accessible schedule.
 | E-E02, J-J-T03, J-J-T04, J-J-T05, J-J-T08
SG-FLOOR, SG-LOAD, SG-JOINT, SG-TEST
 | HZ-14 · Accessible body and boarding loads are inferred from regular car
PK-03, PK-11, PK-14
 | Stage2 accessible31display objects/28in floor and regular110-member/64hardpoint structure are different incomplete representations. Wheelchair/lift/door/companion loads and evacuation can bypass unqualified structure.
 | Separate actual accessible support/door-frame/securement and occupied circulation, accepted floor states and real boarding/rescue equipment; retain11fixed+2chair layout as trial geometry, not rated occupancy.
 | E-E01, E-E02, E-E13, E-E14, J-J-T05, J-J-T11

 | HZ-15 · Door/glazing/body/roof failure obstructs egress or injures people

 | E-E11, E-E12, E-E13, E-E14, J-J-T11

PK-07, PK-09, PK-13

 | Design actual enclosure/cooling/fuel/fire/exhaust routing and both-direction occupied intake separation; accepted material/egress/detection and incident plan. No universal separation distance or historical-shell construction is inferred.

3S.3 · OPERATING / MAINTENANCE / EMERGENCY DEVELOPMENT · 19 OCTOBER 2026
Define the working rules before anyone operates the train.
10conditional procedures,8functional modes,6responsible functions,8energy-control subjects and10maintenance families for the actual proposed custom train. This is a development basis—not an approved driver manual, control implementation or permission to operate. No cab/valve positions, pneumatic settings, caging steps, field-test speeds or numerical rejection limits are invented.
Current state: unreleased development. Actual construction, controls, role qualifications, accepted limits, survey/authority evidence and commissioning remain open. A missing critical procedure/limit is no-dispatch, not an invitation to choose a value on site.
Stored energy: exhausting air does not remove spring-chamber force or loaded suspension/body energy. Control buttons/E-stop are not physical all-source isolation. Qualified specialists must define actual restraint, lifting/support and equipment-specific isolation; no universal vent-all-air or spring-caging sequence is provided.
Preopening checksBoarding / dispatchEmergency / evacuationReturn to serviceEditable10procedure basis
Roles must be covered—not assumed to be one person
 | ID
 | Function
 | Development responsibility
 | RL-01
 | Responsible owner/operating manager
 | Own controlled configuration/manual, appoint qualified people, maintain authority/insurance records and prohibit dispatch on unresolved critical defects.
 | RL-02
 | Designated driver
 | Control train only under accepted operating mode, acknowledge dispatch/stop communication, verify vehicle readiness and stop/no-dispatch on disagreement. Brake controls and limits must be trained on the actual accepted train.
 | RL-03
 | Conductor / boarding lead
 | Coordinate passenger count and accessible assistance, clear doors/platforms/stairs, maintain passenger protection and give the single defined boarding-clear confirmation. Not automatically the driver.
 | RL-04
 | Route / station dispatch function
 | Protect route occupancy, crossings, station and work limits; confirm route availability and record movements. Future second-train operation requires a separate accepted separation design.
 | RL-05
 | Qualified maintenance and energy-control lead
 | Control defect isolation, lifting/restraint/energy-specific service, configuration and restoration. Specialist wheel/axle, spring-brake, structural/NDT and electrical tasks retain their qualified personnel.
 | RL-06
 | Incident / rescue lead and trained responders
 | Coordinate protected site/evacuation/rescue decisions, emergency services access, communications and accounting. Exact rescue method/equipment and assistance training must be accepted before passenger use.
No specific staff count or people are assigned. Combining roles needs an accepted visibility/workload/communication assessment; driver, boarding, route protection and rescue functions must remain covered.
Functional modes—not switch positions
 | ID
 | Mode
 | Entry basis
 | Prohibition
 | MD-00
 | Unreleased development
 | Current project state: no actual released passenger train. Nonstructural mockups/research only within their specific safe scope.
 | No passenger/field operation from this Site.
 | MD-01
 | Out of service / protected work
 | Recorded defect or maintenance; protected track/work area, independent vehicle restraint and energy-specific isolation accepted for task.
 | No dispatch; only separately authorized guarded work/test mode.
 | MD-02
 | Secured inspection / charging
 | Actual configured consist restrained, people clear, protected route and approved charge/functional-check procedure.
 | No automatic traction permission from charging or static pressure.
 | MD-03
 | Secured boarding
 | At accepted interface, stopped and physically held by accepted parking/holding arrangement; route and passenger area protected.
 | No departure with any door/gate/stair/lift unsecured, unclear boarding signal or unresolved fault.
 | MD-04
 | Authorized normal run
 | Accepted route/mode/direction, every-vehicle brake release/reserve/continuity and boarding clear; designated driver and dispatch functions confirm.
 | Any credible stop request, readiness loss or route/communication conflict removes dispatch permission under accepted response.
 | MD-05
 | Stopped incident / emergency
 | Accepted stop response invoked; protect route and people, assess whether remaining aboard or evacuation is safer. Verify and maintain accepted holding/restraint for controlled assisted unloading, with actual changing floor/suspension state; urgent fire/smoke escape follows its separate accepted emergency priority.
 | No reset-and-go, generic caging/towing or unapproved movement to a station.
 | MD-06
 | Specialist rescue / recovery
 | Separate engineered plan, independent restraint, qualified people/equipment and protected route; actual isolated brakes and vehicle damage recorded.
 | No passenger-service permission and no improvised rerailing/lifting/pushing.
 | MD-07
 | Return-to-service hold
 | Repairs/configuration/inspection and required regression complete, actual brakes restored, accountable signoffs and actual readiness rechecked.
 | Only specifically accepted configuration may re-enter inspection/boarding/run modes.
OP-01 · CONDITIONAL DEVELOPMENT, NOT FIELD-READY
Before first service / after material change
Trigger: Initial operation, route change, new consist or altered hardware/process/software/repair.
Roles: RL-01, RL-02, RL-04, RL-05
Match actual serial/revision/consist/route to signed accepted configuration and manuals; close applicable design/manufacturing/site/authority/test gates for this use.
Review S2 change-impact cases and documented regression decision; no automatic transfer of prototype or trailer evidence.
Appoint qualified roles, inspect protected route/stations and verify emergency access/equipment/communications. Accepted controls/limits/defect criteria and shift checks must be present before authorization.
Hold: Missing governing approval, actual control procedure, critical limit, qualified staffing or accepted test evidence means no operation.
Reuse: 3E As_Built_Configuration_Register; Deviation_Impact_and_Regression; Test_Readiness_and_Witness; 3S.2 CC-01..08 and SQ-11
OP-02 · CONDITIONAL DEVELOPMENT, NOT FIELD-READY
Preopening / after interruption inspection
Trigger: Proposed preopening each operating day, and after weather/impact/repair/extended interruption as accepted by the responsible engineer.
Roles: RL-02, RL-03, RL-04, RL-05
Inspect actual route, drainage, formation/fasteners/joints, crossings/turnouts/protection, clearance and stations against accepted survey/track limits; record defects and protect work limits.
Inspect every vehicle wheel/axlebox/suspension/retention, couplings and guards, D1 hub/carrier/cam/chamber/hoses, body/doors/platform/stairs and accessible equipment using safe visible access and the accepted inspection plan.
Verify fluid/fuel/exhaust/cooling and electrical/HVAC/emergency/communication condition; complete protected charging and every-vehicle brake application/release/readiness checks using approved methods. Record actual observations, not a pressure-only pass.
Hold: Any structural/running-gear/brake/track/boarding uncertainty or missing accepted inspection limit is no-dispatch until qualified disposition.
Reuse: 3E configuration/readiness/instrumentation/measurement records; 3S.2 E01 E04 E10 E11 E12 and K-ST01/10/18/19
OP-03 · CONDITIONAL DEVELOPMENT, NOT FIELD-READY
Secured boarding and departure authorization
Trigger: Each departure and each boarding event at origin orNorthPole.
Roles: RL-02, RL-03, RL-04
Use only the accepted stopped boarding interface and actual holding state; protect people from moving vehicles, couplings, pinch zones and adjacent track.
Board within accepted occupancy/seat/wheelchair/companion arrangement. Trained assistance follows the accepted accessible equipment/securement/evacuation plan; no improvised carrying or mobility-device substitution.
Confirm passenger count, designated seated positions and clearance; positively stow/secure actual doors/gates/stairs/lift/bridge and verify physical status, not an assumed indicator alone.
Boarding lead gives the one defined clear signal; dispatch confirms protected route; driver confirms every-vehicle brake reserve/release/continuity and accepted run state. No movement on a conflicting or missing confirmation.
Hold: Open/unsecured equipment, unclear status/communication, inadequate holding or inaccessible accepted boarding solution means hold boarding/departure.
Reuse: 3E Event_Timing_Record and configuration record; 3S.2 E13/E14, K-ST01/19
OP-04 · CONDITIONAL DEVELOPMENT, NOT FIELD-READY
Normal running, stopping and station arrival
Trigger: Only after actual passenger operation is accepted for the specific route/direction/consist.
Roles: RL-02, RL-03, RL-04
Use accepted speed/direction/sight-distance and route occupancy rules; this document supplies no mph or minimum headway.
Monitor actual brake/power readiness, wheel/vehicle condition and communications. A staff stop request must have a defined understood response; lost/ambiguous route permission is not permission to continue.
Arrive using the validated braking/control procedure, establish accepted stopped holding and route protection, then permit boarding equipment/doors to be used. A drivetrain or hydrostatic resistance is not credited as parking.
Hold: No routine pushing/reversing/run-around/towing or second-train service from a locomotive reverser or from this normal-operation outline.
Reuse: 3E Event_Timing/Run_Measurement records; 3S.2 E07/08/09, K-ST16;3R.2 topology
OP-05 · CONDITIONAL DEVELOPMENT, NOT FIELD-READY
Fault stop, report and dispatch lockout
Trigger: Readiness alarm/disagreement, leakage, unexpected brake drag, response loss, strike/derailment indication or credible unsafe condition.
Roles: RL-02, RL-03, RL-04, RL-05
Invoke the actual approved stop response and protect route/people; notify dispatch/boarding/maintenance through the defined communication chain.
Record time/location, exact vehicle/component, alarms/pressures/mechanical behavior, passengers and relevant environment without entering an unsafe zone.
Place affected train/track out of service pending qualified diagnosis. Preserve evidence and avoid repetitive resets or fault clearing that hides the cause.
Hold: No bridged interlock, repeated-reset dispatch, pressure-only assurance, release of an isolated spring brake, or unapproved passenger movement on remaining brakes.
Reuse: 3E deviation/regression and long-format measurement records; 3S.2 E04..09/E12; K-ST05..20
OP-06 · CONDITIONAL DEVELOPMENT, NOT FIELD-READY
Parking, end of shift and storage
Trigger: End of service, unattended storage or maintenance handover.
Roles: RL-02, RL-04, RL-05
Use accepted storage track, protected route and actual stopped parking/independent restraint sequence; verify holding physically using the approved method.
Record consist, brake/energy status and outstanding defects. Secure controls/access, maintain required emergency/fire protection and perform actual OEM/engineer shutdown and weather protection.
Do not disconnect power, vent reservoirs or uncouple until the accepted sequence proves restraint and people are clear; spring air loss can change brake forces and is not a universal shutdown instruction.
Hold: No assumed0%survey or hydrostatic holding; no unattended vehicle with unproved parking, caged brake or unknown restraint state.
Reuse: 3E As_Built_Configuration and deviation records; 3S.2 E06/K-ST04/09/15
OP-07 · CONDITIONAL DEVELOPMENT, NOT FIELD-READY
Protected maintenance and hazardous-energy control
Trigger: Service, access under/within train, component removal or adjustment.
Roles: RL-05, RL-04
Qualified lead identifies the exact task and all possible energy/reaccumulation sources; accepted vehicle restraint, track protection and lifting/support points precede work.
Use the accepted equipment-specific isolation/blocking/verification procedure and controlled lock transfer. Cab buttons, an E-stop or a software flag are not physical isolation.
Control air, electrical/charger/shore, engine fuel/start, drive pressure/inertia, loaded springs and gravity separately. Gauge zero or an empty air reservoir does not remove stored spring or suspended-body energy.
Maintain verification and work boundaries through the task; use only accepted joint/material/process/OEM service documents. Spring-brake chamber internals are not farm fabrication/service work.
Hold: No generic vent-all-air, spring-chamber opening/caging, lifting or underbody access procedure is provided. Missing exact isolation/restraint/support evidence means stop work.
Reuse: 3F Material_Traceability; Weld_Joint_Release; Prototype_Fabrication_Traveler; 3J traveler;3S.2 change-impact record
OP-08 · CONDITIONAL DEVELOPMENT, NOT FIELD-READY
Emergency protection, assistance and evacuation
Trigger: Fire/smoke, medical issue, loss of movement/power, blocked route/exit or other emergency.
Roles: RL-02, RL-03, RL-04, RL-06
Use the accepted emergency stop/route protection and incident communications; summon emergency services when needed and identify actual accessible route/location for responders.
Incident lead assesses immediate danger and whether remaining aboard or controlled evacuation offers lower risk; do not automatically keep people aboard in fire/smoke or evacuate into moving-track/terrain hazards.
For controlled assisted unloading or bridge/lift use, verify and maintain the accepted vehicle holding/independent restraint arrangement as applicable; account for floor/suspension movement while passengers leave. The accepted emergency plan must separately prioritize immediate escape from fire/smoke without a blanket restraint-verification delay.
Trained staff account for passengers, mobility needs and companions; use only qualified deployed exits/equipment and the accepted off-station rescue plan. Maintain lighting, guarded paths and accessible assistance without improvised carrying/transfer.
Keep other movements/work isolated; preserve route/rescue access. Reunification/accounting and medical handover follow the actual accepted site plan.
Hold: Do not rely solely on moving back to a station. No staff lifting/carrying, improvised bridge, unqualified lift or routine pushing authority is created here.
Reuse: 3E mockup/observation and E13/E14 installed exercise records; 3S.2 E12/13/14 and actual accepted emergency plan
OP-09 · CONDITIONAL DEVELOPMENT, NOT FIELD-READY
Specialist rescue, towing or derailment recovery
Trigger: A disabled or displaced vehicle cannot be restored by normal accepted operation.
Roles: RL-01, RL-04, RL-05, RL-06
Protect all route/work zones and independently restrain actual vehicles before any brake/coupling/energy change. Assess passenger evacuation separately from recovery.
Qualified responsible professionals approve the exact rescue consist, direction, brake command/continuity, coupling/compression, traction/visibility, damaged wheel/track and lifting/rerailing plan before movement.
Any OEM-specific mechanical spring release is performed only by qualified personnel under that independently restrained procedure; record each disabled/caged brake and its effect. No operational holding credit remains for a mechanically disabled spring.
After recovery, quarantine damage/changed configuration until inspections, repairs and documented regression/return-to-service release are complete.
Hold: No improvised farm-tractor tow, chain substitution, jacking/rerailing point, caging instruction or passenger movement with disabled brakes is authorized.
Reuse: 3E configuration/deviation/readiness records; 3S.2 E06/E08, K-ST07/15/16/18 and CC-01..08
OP-10 · CONDITIONAL DEVELOPMENT, NOT FIELD-READY
Return to service after repair, release/caging or change
Trigger: After any defect, maintenance isolation, repair, mechanical brake release, recoupling or configuration change.
Roles: RL-01, RL-02, RL-04, RL-05
Document exact repair/configuration and accountable inspection; verify every removed part/guard/retainer/fastener, coupling/trainline and disabled brake is restored to the accepted build.
Qualified energy-control lead verifies work area/people/tools and actual equipment integrity; remove locks/restraints only by the accepted responsible handover procedure. Energization for guarded tests is not passenger dispatch.
Complete documented S2 impact assessment and required approved regression tests; every vehicle must show actual application/release/readiness after reconnection or brake work. Resolve anomalies and retain raw evidence.
Operating manager accepts only the specifically released configuration and qualified staff/route. Re-enter preopening/boarding checks; never treat an indicator reset or publication as return-to-service approval.
Hold: Unrestored caging/isolated brake, unknown defect cause, unapproved deviation, missing inspection or failed/absent required regression keeps the train out of service.
Reuse: 3E configuration/deviation/evidence acceptance/readiness forms; 3S.2 CC-01..08 and SQ-11
Task-specific energy and defect controls
 | ID
 | Energy
 | Hazard
 | Definition required
 | Prohibition
 | EN-01
 | Vehicle motion / gravity
 | Train can roll, separate or shift even with0%target; suspension/body/lift loads can fall.
 | Accepted independent rail-vehicle restraint, track protection and engineered lift/support points; task-specific stability.
 | Brake command or estimated grade is not independent blocking.
 | EN-02
 | Spring brake stored force
 | Combination chamber spring remains hazardous without air.
 | Exact delivered OEM qualified service/replacement and independently restrained rescue method if required.
 | Do not open spring chamber or infer safe force from zero gauge pressure.
 | EN-03
 | Air reservoirs / isolated lines
 | Check valves/closed cocks can trap pressure or allow reaccumulation.
 | Actual tagged circuit/isolation/verification and pressure-vessel/brake protection basis.
 | No universal drain/vent sequence or assumed single-gauge isolation.
 | EN-04
 | Suspension / sidebearer / loaded mechanism
 | Loaded coils, bump stops, sidebearers, stairs/lifts and body supports can store force or move.
 | Actual unloading/restraint/support method for selected geometry and task.
 | Do not remove a mount, guide, torque link or stop under uncharacterized load.
 | EN-05
 | Electrical AC/DC / shore / battery
 | Multiple sources, charger/generator backfeed and stored electrical energy.
 | Electrical specialist establishes disconnect/absence-of-energy, discharge, grounding/bonding and test precautions.
 | Software off, disconnected low-voltage control or E-stop is not all-source isolation.
 | EN-06
 | Diesel start, fuel and hot exhaust
 | Unexpected start, fuel/fire/chemical exposure, hot cooling/exhaust/DPF.
 | Exact engine shutdown/start isolation/cooldown/fuel/fire and exhaust service methods.
 | No homemade fuel/exhaust/DPF bypass or occupied-cabin exposure assumption.
 | EN-07
 | Hydrostatic propulsion pressure and rotating drive
 | Closed-loop/charge pressure, accumulator if actually fitted, inertia and mechanically coupled axles.
 | Actual OEM drive circuit and supported shaft/gear energy isolation; restrained rotating hardware.
 | Hydraulic propulsion is not the superseded hydraulic coach-brake system.
 | EN-08
 | Test fixture / applied loads
 | Ballast, actuators, restrained springs/pressure and lifted equipment store energy.
 | Approved guarded fixture/load path/containment, calibrated instruments, abort/rescue plan and qualified director.
 | No test from a crosswalk or blank result sheet alone.
Any unassessed safety-related wheel/axle/bearing/suspension/brake/structure/track/boarding/power condition remains out of service pending qualified disposition. No passenger degraded mode is accepted. Never bypass a safety interlock or repeatedly reset a fault to dispatch.Energy / no-dispatch register

OPS-S01 · Dexter · 9–15K trailer running-gear service manual LIT-006-00
Checked19October2026UTC. PDF43, Air Brake Components / Spring Brake; PDF1 introduction and5service notice. Manufacturer identifies stored spring hazard and qualified service; manual is for trailer running gear.
Not a rail maintenance schedule, rail approval, exact delivered chamber manual or selected pressure/stroke specification. No trailer mileage, wheel-nut procedure,130psi caution value or generic stroke table is adopted.
OPS-S02 · US OSHA · 29CFR1910.147 control of hazardous energy
Checked19October2026UTC. (a)(1)(ii)(A), (b), (c)(4), (d)(5)-(6), (e). Scope excludes construction/agriculture employment; control-circuit buttons are not energy isolators. Energy-specific isolation and stored-energy verification are addressed.
Method reference only. Employment classification, Nevada enforcement and applicable construction/agriculture/general-industry obligations require competent determination; no farm exemption or automatic applicability is asserted.
OPS-S03 · US OSHA · 29CFR1910.169 air receivers
Checked19October2026UTC. (a)(1). Section explicitly is not intended for compressed-air machinery/equipment on transportation vehicles.
Do not import stationary-receiver rules or relief percentages as this train reservoir specification. Actual pressure-vessel/brake approval and governing jurisdiction remain open.
These scope checks prevent applying trailer/stationary-machine instructions as accepted rail requirements. Responsible authorities/professionals must determine actual jurisdiction and adopted requirements. No new applicability approval is claimed.

3S.2 · CURRENT-CONFIGURATION TEST / TRAVELER CROSSWALK · 19 OCTOBER 2026
Do not run the old hydraulic test as a D1 air-brake test.
Existing3E recordE03 explicitly says hydraulic actuator response, blocked drain and auxiliary release. It is preserved as predecessor evidence but superseded for the current D1 configuration. An air-brake pressure label cannot repair the missing method, fixture, torque/stroke curves or spring-force/anti-compounding evidence.
This issue reconciles59existing records:14Ecommissioning subjects,12Jprototype subjects,20Kfunctional brake states and13Jtraveler steps. It adds specific current-use dispositions, a12-step evidence release sequence and8change-impact cases. It does not generate another set of blank forms or report performed tests.
All approved protocols, numerical acceptance/abort limits, results and witness records remain unset. No test is ready to execute from this crosswalk. No pneumatic pressure, spring-caging procedure, proof load, dynamic test speed or fabrication permission is assigned.

Manufacturing, installation and commissioning evidence order
Gate links identify related evidence subjects, not a demand to close every train-wide approval before separately authorized coupon/component qualification. Each scoped test needs its own accepted configuration, fixture, protocol, limits and responsible release; its success cannot release the full train.
Farm crew can perform released preparation, fitting, finishing, routing support and track work under the relevant specialist plan. Safety-critical machining/press fits, structural joint/process release, stored-energy test fixtures, electrical protection and vehicle/track acceptance require qualified specialists. This sequence does not authorize those activities. Research/survey preparation may overlap; no durations or procurement commitments are invented.
SQ-00 · Scope, applicability and accountable design roles
Record exact intended attraction/vehicles/site use and applicable approvals; establish named design/acceptance roles. Prepare unsent technical requests only.
Responsible: Owner + responsible authorities/engineers. Stop: No presumed FRA/amusement/building exemption or shop/operation permission.
SQ-01 · Ground-level occupied layout review
Use existing3E regular/accessible layouts, six-adult review and real mobility devices; record coats/knees/door hardware/turning/companion and supervised evacuation-layout observations.
Responsible: Owner/crew under qualified mockup plan. Stop: Mockup is nonstructural and not installed lift/stair or vehicle acceptance.
SQ-02 · Controlled physical interface definition
Freeze actual source geometry and loads/motions, supported fits/joints, component revisions and service envelopes; coordinate custom whole truck, coach and locomotive.
Responsible: Responsible subsystem engineers + component suppliers. Stop: No guessed mount sizes or selected-spring claims from sensitivity inputs.
SQ-03 · Released coupon / fixture / manufacturing plan
Apply J-TR01..09 to accepted exact production-material specimens/fixtures; material traceability, process qualification, fit-up/weld/NDT hold points and approved guarded test plan.
Responsible: Structural/welding engineers + qualified fabricator/inspector. Stop: No structural load or stored-energy test before protocol/limits/fixture authorization.
SQ-04 · Component and connection qualification
Accepted exact brake maps, wheel/axle/hub manufacture/retention evidence, seat/floor coupons and articulated coupling/guide/support joints; retain anomalies and revisions.
Responsible: Responsible test directors + witnesses. Stop: Proof test does not establish fatigue life or release other geometry.
SQ-05 · Complete first custom truck and frame
Integrate actual wheels/axleboxes/springs/dampers/guides/stops/pivot/sidebearers/D1supports, revised frame details and full inspection; validate wheel loads/twist/motion and service access.
Responsible: Vehicle/running-gear engineers + qualified fabricator. Stop: DisplayG1 and J1a member-only model do not satisfy this stage.
SQ-06 · Integrated first regular and accessible vehicles
Separate body/support/layout configurations; route actual hoses/cables/guards, emergency exits/lighting, roof equipment and boarding mechanisms; capture as-built mass/CG and datums.
Responsible: Body/boarding/brake/electrical engineers + qualified installers. Stop: Accessible coach requires its own installation/structural acceptance, not regular-car inference.
SQ-07 · Site proof section and station interfaces
Approve actual profile/formation/drainage/track/joint/fastening section and stations. Crew records each released installation hold point; survey measured as-built geometry and correct defects.
Responsible: Survey/civil/track/boarding professionals + supervised farm crew. Stop: Do not lay selected rail/joints or set station level from unaccepted CAD/floor datum.
SQ-08 · Secured pneumatic, power and control integration
Revised E03..06/E10..12 and20Kstates under approved safe fixtures; actual complete air/power configuration, reserve/timing/separation/anti-compounding and return-to-safe state.
Responsible: Brake/control/electrical specialists + test director. Stop: No live hose cutting, unrestrained spring release or arbitrary field fault injection.
SQ-09 · Controlled unoccupied vehicle and full-consist trials
Before full-consist trials, all3regular coaches plus1accessible coach and locomotive need separately released fabrication/configuration and qualified as-built evidence, including the two additional regular coaches not covered by the first-coach stage. Engineer-approved tare/ballast sequence and containment, stopping/holding/adhesion/thermal/air-duty, drawbar/articulation/wheel-load and drive cooling/exhaust tests; actual every-vehicle records.
Responsible: Vehicle/track/brake engineers + qualified operators/witnesses. Stop: No passengers or public operation; accepted limits/abort/directions/degraded states must be explicit. First-coach qualification does not release repeat vehicles.
SQ-10 · Installed boarding and supervised emergency exercises
E14 installed equipment qualification first, then E13 participant exercise under approved protocol, actual both stations and off-station rescue access/communications.
Responsible: Accessibility/vehicle/boarding professionals + responders/operator. Stop: No participant loading of unqualified equipment or unsafe injected failure.
SQ-11 · Configuration freeze and conditional operational handover
Disposition actual evidence and all applicable critical gates; approved manuals/staff/training/inspection/maintenance/defect limits, route dispatch, emergency and signed acceptance records.
Responsible: Owner/operator + responsible engineers/authorities/insurer roles. Stop: Publication is not release. Repeat fleet fabrication and second-train operation require separate configuration/review permissions.Editable release sequence
Changes that require a documented regression decision
The records below are subjects to review, not instructions to run every listed test. The responsible engineer identifies affected evidence, reanalysis, inspections and safe requalification before a changed configuration is accepted. Accepted old evidence does not automatically transfer to a new material, joint, component, mass or operating mode.

Records and measurement discipline
Reuse3E configuration, deviation/regression, readiness/witness, event timing and long-format measurement records;3F material/weld/traveler and systems return forms;3J specimen/test records. Record exact vehicle and component serial/revision, loads, pressure/mechanical response, environment, instruments/range/calibration/uncertainty and common time basis. Keep raw data and anomalies; a blank or null result is not zero or pass. Original instrumentation sampling targets are planning inputs, not adopted acceptance limits.
Engineer-defined observability must cover each relevant vehicle/critical pressure and mechanical path, including the unresolved locomotive package. Pressure alone does not prove brake torque, release or parking. Digital state alone does not prove physical fault response. No body-mounted pressure gauge substitutes for actual wheel-slide/load/temperature/force evidence when required by the protocol.Exact inherited source members and current evidence

3R.1 · SURVEY / TRACK / STATION INTERFACES · 19 OCTOBER 2026
A zero-grade target needs measured track geometry.
Completed independent data/specification/tool issue, not a surveyed alignment, track construction drawing or complete3R. No property coordinates, terrain, accepted track tolerances or NorthPole location have been invented.36in remains an evaluation gauge.
3R.2 · REAL RAIL DATA / TOPOLOGY / PROCUREMENT · 19 OCTOBER 2026
The linked rail CAD does not match its section drawing.
The supplier’s linked30ASCE paper section3040 has79.375mm height and base width. The linkedIGES imports with 88.9 ×88.9mm face-envelope and304.8mm segment length:9.525mm, or12%, larger in height/base. SourceCMunits and importerMMunits were checked; both face-only and complete-shape extents agree. This is not a rolling tolerance adjustment.
The40-root/40-face, zero-solid surface model is kernel-valid, but that does not establish component identity. It is excluded from the project’s accepted geometry. No alternative rail family is asserted and it is not scaled, renamed or put into a rotatable train model as30ASCE. Exact source hashes, units, locators and an editable read-only inspection script are retained.
Joint data also differ: Kimes narrative5/8×2¾inbolt; its table/drawing5/8×3in; Harmer’s historical set5/8×2½in, with a different bar length. These are not interchangeable assemblies merely because all mention30ASCE. Supplier-confirmed mating faces, hole/grip/thread and joint details are required before ordering or drilling.

Bounded rail-support demand—not a rail or tie-spacing approval
The paper30ASCE reference gives I=4.10in⁴ and head/base section moduli2.55/2.70in³. These are nominal geometry, not a rail axle-load rating. The coach upper allocations13,200kg regular and11,000kg accessible, divided equally over8wheels, give16.181/13.484kN static wheel probes. Actual wheel imbalance and the unselected locomotive axle arrangement may govern.
Source computes18ideal cases:2coach allocations ×3unaccepted load factors ×3unaccepted spans. At the0.6m span probe below, a centered point load acts on an ideal simply supported bay. M=Pℓ/4; σ=M/Z; δ=Pℓ³/(48EI), with assumed E=200GPa. Paper section properties are used—not the mismatchedIGES.

Routing recommendation remains conditional on real terrain
Study lead: a continuous one-direction loop, keeping the locomotive leading in normal guest service through both origin andNorthPole stops. This is a technical operating preference, not a final property route. Survey and accepted curves, stations, drainage and access must establish feasibility.
 | Topology
 | Normal movement
 | Condition before selection
 | Interfaces to engineer
 | TO-A
Continuous one-direction loop
 | Locomotive leads the same consist around a continuous route through origin andNorthPole stops.
 | Study lead only if actual boundary/terrain can support a0%route with accepted curves, two boarding locations and emergency/maintenance access.
 | Protected stops and occupancy/dispatch; future second train still needs engineered separation and blocked-route response.
 | TO-B
Point-to-point with terminal balloon loops
 | Train turns on track at each end and locomotive leads both travel directions on the shared stem.
 | Requires actual land and acceptable curve/swept envelopes at BOTHends, plus engineered turnouts and route protection. No minimum radius or loop footprint is selected.
 | Opposing movements on common track, point position/locking and fouling protection; no default trailing-point movement.
 | TO-C
Point-to-point reversing / push-pull
 | Whole consist reverses; locomotive at one end pushes on one leg unless another accepted drive/control arrangement is developed.
 | Not cleared for normal service: leading-end visibility/control, braking in both directions, compressive coupling stability and protected doors/platforms need new integrated validation.
 | Accepted leading-end control and communication; reversal/rollback/retarding behavior and trainline failure cases. A diesel reverser alone does not qualify a pushed passenger consist.
 | TO-D
Locomotive run-around
 | Locomotive disconnects, changes end using additional track and reconnects.
 | Not preferred normal guest cycle: extra movements, turnouts, physical couplings/hoses, positive coach restraint and post-reconnection checks require an approved operating case.
 | Controlled uncoupling/air isolation/parking/recharge/brake continuity; no assumption that passengers may remain aboard during shunting.
Off-station evacuation cannot depend solely on recovery to a station. Fire, blocked route or traction/brake failure can prevent movement. Retain independent rescue access and qualified procedures. Later second-train use requires accepted route occupancy, separation and fault-response design; no timetable or collision-protection performance is assumed.Topology / reversing / recovery decision record

Review track-reference data here
Choose a completed JSON exchange file. Processing stays in this browser; this checker makes no upload and does not save the data to the Site. It reports arithmetic and missing metadata—not permission to build or operate. Blank templates deliberately contain no survey points. The optional example is synthetic software test data, not this farm or a proposed route.

What the measurements mean
Average track-reference grade is100Δz/Δhorizontal-chainage. Paired rail points also report each rail’s elevation change per CENTERLINE chainage and right-minus-left crosslevel inmm. On curves these are not each rail’s own running-length grade. Adjacent-sample twist isΔcrosslevel/Δchainage, inmm/m. Flat mean rail elevation can coexist with changing crosslevel;0%longitudinal target does not eliminate track twist, suspension articulation or wheel-load checks.
Railhead survey-point spacing is not track gauge. Gauge needs a separate running-face measurement at the engineer’s defined depth/profile. The tool never substitutes the coordinate baseline for gauge. Three-point radii are sample circumcircles, not a designed transition, an accepted minimum or proof of clearance between samples. Twist is not automatically evaluated on the eventual truck wheelbase.
Declare increasing-station direction and equivalent left/right rail references, exact units, ground-distance basis, vertical datum and control. Raw ellipsoidal heights, undeclared grid-to-ground scale, mixed foot definitions or nonincreasing chainage are not silently repaired. Survey uncertainty and observation density still govern what defects can be resolved.
Accepted grade/gauge/crosslevel/twist limits and actual truck-base checks remain null. Data quality “analyzable” means only that these calculations can run. No rail section, tie spacing, ballast thickness, drainage section, turnout, curve or station elevation is selected by this tool.

3P.1 · CURRENT-CONFIGURATION RECONCILIATION · 19 OCTOBER 2026
Hotel power, cabin heat and air brakes: one coordinated budget.
Completed audit/calculation issue, not completed Phase3P or an electrical installation drawing. Exact original3F inputs, calculator and results are preserved and hashed. 168 numeric/component comparisons replay the original six thermal cases within floating-point precision.
3P.3 · OWNER MINI-SPLIT PLACEMENT · 19 OCTOBER 2026
Indoor head above the end door; outdoor unit on the non-boarding side.
This is the current HVAC design direction for all four coaches. Use reversible heat-pump mini-splits for heating and cooling. The outside section contains the compressor and fan. Keep each matched refrigeration system on its own coach; carry electrical power between coaches.
Recommended supply:120/240V,60Hz. Use240V for the larger mini-splits and120V for suitable lights/outlets/auxiliaries. Small115V systems exist, but voltage does not establish adequate occupied capacity. The final generator rating remains open until actual HVAC, ventilation, brake-compressor and auxiliary demand is coordinated.
The locomotive/service enclosure is the candidate location for one dedicated diesel hotel generator, subject to space, mass, cooling and exhaust review. It is separate from traction in this working arrangement. A main-engine-driven generator remains an alternative requiring low-speed/idle power regulation analysis.
Above-door clearance must be designed.
The inherited clear interior height is86.61in. A documented Mitsubishi18k-class indoor unit is12in high, leaving only74.61in beneath it even with zero upper gap. That is a packaging warning, not an accepted door height or installation.
Preserve the intended door/circulation clearance and resolve a raised end-roof/header pocket or exact head placement with the real installation manual. Unit width, intake, louvers, opening cover and filter-removal travel all need space. One head at a chosen end is the initial arrangement; final capacity and air throw may require additional heads, potentially above both end doors. No head count, bracket or roof change is dimensioned for fabrication yet.
Outdoor unit side remains defined relative to the boarding side. Confirm it stays clear of boarding at both stations, including reversing, curves, body roll and maintenance access. Its carrier attaches to the coach structure and contributes weight and lateral load; it does not mount to a moving axlebox.
Existing load estimates constrain the equipment choice.
These retainedP1 sensitivity cases useU1.5W/m²K,420W cabin equipment heat,33regular/14accessible thermal occupants, assumed7.5L/s/person outside air and averaged door leakage. Summer110°F/no-solar is a stress case, not a Christmas-evening forecast. Actual weather, envelope and ventilation basis remain unaccepted.

One nominal12,000BTU/h system provides about3.52kW of cooling. It cannot be presumed sufficient for these occupied loads. A matched heat pump must also deliver the necessary heating at the selected outdoor temperature, including defrost effects; warmup needs body thermal mass/time, not just steady heat loss.
Across all four cars, the retained110°F evening case at assumedCOP2.5 gives26.79kW including4.32kW of allocated auxiliaries, before brake compressor, other missing loads, transients, derating and reserve. This is conditional arithmetic, not a selected generator. Lower winter duty may require less; electrical input is not thermal capacity.
At equal power factor, a2.4kW load draws20A at120V or10A at240V. Higher voltage reduces current, not energy or generatorkW. The main feeder must still support balanced120V loads, neutral and protective grounding.
Installation interfaces to carry into drawings
Above-door indoor mounting: Attach OEM plate and positive vibration restraint to engineered end-wall framing; preserve intake/filter/front-cover service, throw down aisle, door swing, circulation and accessible controls. Exact hardware/manual and dynamic loads determine fasteners and reinforcement.
Non-boarding-side outdoor mounting: Mount on coach structure with a defined load path, vibration isolators/positive retention and outward free airflow. Guard without blocking coil/fan/service. Add actual mass/CG and lateral moment to frame/truck ledger; verify both stations and curve swept envelope/roll.
Refrigerant and controls: Keep both units on same sprung coach; use approved refrigerant, line lengths/diameters, pressure rating, supported protected routing, movement relief, flare/braze/testing per exact OEM procedure and refrigerant-qualified installer. No guessed flexible hose or suspension-crossing copper.
Condensate and defrost water: Provide accessible drains with maintained fall over accepted parked/running pitch/roll, cleanout and suitable approved pump only if needed. Keep water/ice off rails, brake equipment, stairs and boarding route; evaluate outdoor heat-pump defrost drainage.
Fresh air and comfort: Ordinary recirculating mini-split heads do not satisfy the existing outside-air requirement by themselves. Retain a separate engineered ventilation path and coordinate its heat/moisture/fan loads. Verify end-to-end occupied temperature and airflow with doors opening.
Generation and distribution: Preliminary120/240V four-conductor feeder L1/L2/N/PE,120V loads balanced between legs and appropriate240V branches. Final protection, disconnects, cable/jumper flex/retention, bonding/neutral/shore transfer, selectivity and voltage-drop design by electrical specialist. No neutral-to-coach-body bond assumption.
Hotel generator installation: Confirm duty/deratedkW,kVA,PF/harmonic compatibility, simultaneous restart/defrost and voltage/frequency response; sequence HVAC restart. Resolve fuel, cooling/ventilation, acoustics, fire separation, service and exhaust/CO separation from every occupied intake. Verify load bank and actual train loads before acceptance.
Primary sources checked19October2026: Pioneer voltage guidance; Pioneer power-feed guidance; Mitsubishi example dimensions, page2; Cummins commercial-mobile voltage example. None establishes rail application suitability or final selection.

A full-size fit/service mockup verifies access before roof cutting; it does not replace structural or electrical acceptance. Farm crew can assist the reviewed mockup and measurements. Structural, HVAC and electrical specialists own their design, live/refrigerant work and acceptance.

The old hydraulic-pump allowance is not a current train load
Original3F contained two24V/20A hydraulic pumps per coach at10% duty:96W average and960W simultaneous pump running, before other devices. They are excluded from the current air-brake schedule. Older files remain historical evidence. Do not reuse their pump-start or10A-average battery results as current air-brake sizing.
The remaining6A is still only an inherited allowance, not measured or selected air-brake hardware. At the old60% usable-energy probe, one-hour capacity changes mathematically from16.67Ah (10A) to10Ah (6A). Neither is a battery selection. Emergency-light reserve remains separate. Exact current devices, charger losses/recharge, cold/aging, voltage and peaks remain open.
No compressor load is silently set to zero. D1 has32coach combination chambers plus the separately matched locomotive. The saved catalog-volume interface remains33.5607L service and50.3411L spring for the coaches, but its basis is unresolved: those are not accepted free-air consumption or reservoir sizes. Compressor drive/duty/recovery and starting must enter either the traction-auxiliary or electrical budget once, not neither or both. The inherited10kW traction-auxiliary probe does not prove compressor capacity.

Total1080W per coach is an allocation, not actual installed input. The old thermal model used only200W inside-equipment heat. New probes distinguish200W predecessor,420W direct lights/fan/controls into cabin, and1080W if all allocated input ultimately becomes cabin heat. The420W case assumes charger/DC heat is elsewhere; fan work must not already be included in the selected HVAC net-capacity convention. None is accepted cabin heat.
Charger input = operating DC load + battery charging + conversion losses. Place each loss/load at its real location; energy stored in a battery is not instant cabin heat. Do not add the full charger input and downstream DC watts twice. Actual recharge demand may govern generator loading even when cabin heat is lower.

Retain explicit conditions; don’t promote probes into requirements
Basis:3regular ×33 thermal occupants +1accessible ×14 =113thermal people, including one allowance/car. Regular/glass8.6297m²; accessible6.6684m².110°F outside/75°F inside cooling,30°F or20°F outside/68°F inside heating,7.5L/s/person outside air plus0.03m³/s average door leakage, and0.7/1.5/2.5W/m²K opaque-U probes are inherited assumptions, not official site design or adopted ventilation criteria. Humidity and sunlight are separate stresses; evening operation is not assumed identical to solar daytime.
The new source computes18thermal cases and33whole-consist cooling/heating rows. Winter occupied heat subtracts real-location assumed gains; before-gains heat is retained separately. Neither is complete preheat: body thermal mass, starting temperature and allowed warmup time are missing. The2×18×15in gross ducts do not establish net area, filter pressure, sound, fan power or delivered ventilation.
Current primary manual check retains the named Dometic CS variants as comparisons only:15,000Btu/h nominal; FJX3573M compressor13.6/71A rated/locked and fan2.6/6.9A; FJX5557M compressor12.8/57A and fan3.6/8.2A.115V/60Hz single phase. These do not establish watts, generator transients,110°F output, fresh air or rail suitability. Exact supplied revision/nameplate must govern. RV rooftop and rail rooftop/undercar families remain alternatives, not selected installations.
Energy boundaries: the420W probe puts even the platform-light allocation inside, intentionally pending actual placement. Define whether selected HVAC input/COP and net capacity already include fans and controls; do not add the250W ventilation fan twice. Do not put compressor electrical input into cabin gains: condenser heat rejects outside. Transformer losses require their own physical location. The balanced240/480V currents in the source are arithmetic equivalents without transformer loss, not selected wiring topologies.
