Actual brake interface for the custom wheelset study
Checked 19 October 2026 UTC. These are manufacturer dimensions and a proposed fit study, not a released passenger-rail brake assembly.
Primary fit-study candidate: Tolomatic ME220MEFI, part 0745-0024. This is a cast-iron mechanical caliper with machined cam and floating bracket for a fixed 1/2-inch disc. It provides a physical component around which the custom carrier can be drawn while preserving the owner's preference for a commercial combination air/spring chamber. The chamber, force-reduction linkage and brake are not a documented matched assembly.
Verified interface
The OEM floating bracket has two Ø0.386-inch holes at 4.750-inch centers. Draw those two OEM holes at datum coordinates H1=(0,0), H2=(4.750,0) inches. The new carrier's hole clearance, thickness, edge distance, threads, bolts and preload are separate design decisions. The OEM bracket is 7.00 inches wide and 2.92 inches high; its mounting-hole line is 2.17 inches above the lever-axis line. The dimension sheet lists the candidate's A/B/C dimensions as 0.844/3.80/5.98 inches. These are catalogue dimensions, not assigned machining tolerances.
For a 12-inch-diameter packaging study, the OEM table gives D=5.00 inches from disc axis to lever axis and effective braking radius=5.08 inches. Thus the mounting-hole line is 7.17 inches from the axle axis in the depicted radial orientation. This does not define the axial mounting-face datum: use the exact manufacturer CAD or controlled drawing before fixing the carrier standoff. The caliper floating motion accommodates its brake mechanism, not unrestricted wheelset suspension travel.
Sources: ME220 product ↗, OEM dimensional sheet ↗.
Verified force/travel information and integration consequences
The OEM specifications distinguish 580 lbf maximum lever force for aluminum housings and 660 lbf for cast iron. They give dynamic torque in inch-pounds as 7.45 × braking radius in inches × lever force in pounds; static torque uses coefficient 3.725. New total pad clearance is 0.048 inch; maximum axial movement without intermediate adjustment is 0.387 inch; wear before adjustment is 0.104 inch per side. The printed angular-stroke notation requires clarification before conversion.
The drawing shows 4.00-inch and 2.38-inch lever-hole radii. Confirm which input location the catalogue equation covers. Do not apply the rated equation unchanged after moving the pushrod to a different hole. The linked parts sheet explicitly covers die-cast aluminum and gives 580 lbf; this is not a conflict with the cast-iron catalogue rating. Obtain the exact cast-iron variant instructions before accepting 660 lbf in this assembly. It also confirms that wear adjustment is manual.
Sources: OEM specifications ↗, linked parts sheet ↗.
Design implication: a common 30/30 chamber must not be bolted directly to this lever on the assumption that its force is suitable. Match full pressure, maximum parking-spring force, travel, wear and simultaneous service/parking failure loads. A reduction linkage or different chamber may be necessary. Carrier loads include disc reactions and actuator-linkage loads, not only the demanded stopping torque.
Use an axlebox-referenced carrier concept, or another proven means of preserving disc/caliper alignment during primary suspension motion. A torque link may react braking into the bogie, but its pivots, geometry and joint forces remain to be engineered. A rigid chassis bracket with no suspension accommodation is not an acceptable continuation of the current sketch.
A measured alternative, not a change in preference
Twiflex MX13/MX25 calipers accept documented proprietary air thrusters. MXA supplies air-applied service braking; MXK is spring-applied and air-released. DS2501 lists MXA maximum tangential braking force 6.9 kN at 7 bar, based on conditioned pads at nominal friction 0.4. With a 300 mm disc and the manufacturer's effective-radius formula, this implies 807.3 N·m. This is a published operating-condition estimate, not an ultimate carrier load or rail qualification. MXK legacy and current documents differ slightly on the spring force, requiring reconciliation. Neither is a verified substitute for an MGM canister.
Sources: MXA DS2501 ↗, current MX mounting sheet ↗.
The next fabrication development can now include a real mounting pattern, candidate axial envelope, removable carrier arrangement, inspection-access space, custom wheel/axle/rotor interface schedule and force-based carrier calculation. Rotor material, thermal capacity, hub attachment, bearings, axle and wheel fits, fatigue loads, tolerances and brake balance remain coordinated engineering decisions. No stock or application approval has been confirmed, and no supplier has been contacted.

How to read this demonstration
The rails guide the wheelsets while each bogie pivots relative to the coach. Visible axles, suspension and support symbols explain those relationships. Hardware dimensions, ratings, attachments and complete moving clearances are still held.
Play changes between constant-radius geometric poses. It does not simulate a train entering a real transition curve, wheel contact forces, suspension response, braking or derailment resistance. The track remains at the owner's 0% longitudinal-grade target.

The technical source package includes calculation scripts, frozen input data, editable vector/DXF diagrams and a rebuild guide. Phase 3 sheet DXFs use paper inches; the separate coach-envelope DXF uses full-size model inches. The rotating coaches remain the retained Stage 2 space models.Technical source package ↓
CURRENT STUDY BASIS
Dimensions with their limits.
These values coordinate the current study. They are not released manufacturing dimensions or supplier ratings.
Performance screens
Nominal loaded train: 54,755 kg.
145 kg/person stress case: 59,840 kg. Phase 3B upper calculation case: 65,100 kg; not a rated train mass.
Reference calculation speed: 7.5 mph; operating speed remains to be established.
The owner requires 0% longitudinal grade. Verify finished elevations; curve, adhesion and braking limits still depend on actual equipment.
Requirements retained
Diesel propulsion beneath historical styling.
Heating, air conditioning, ventilation and enclosed bodies.
Bench seating without tables or armrests.
Onboard end platforms and protected stairs.
CR-G01: air service brakes and spring-applied, air-released parking are preferred. Common semi-truck combination chambers are inquiry candidates; the complete rail brake system remains to be engineered.
Masses are allocations, not measured equipment weights. Mobility devices are additional to the counted users. Dimensions, capacity and mass must change together when a design decision changes.

BUILD PACKAGE STATUS
What you can review. What still needs design.
The 3L.1 issue adds custom wheelset and brake-carrier interfaces, machining development and a complete fabrication drawing register. Phase 3K adds trainwide air-brake functions, real component evidence, stopping and air budgets, and verification requirements. Phase 3J adds a coordinated J1a member model, global load-path calculations, representative prototype and inspection records. Phase 3I adds dimensioned connection alternatives, reinforced seat/floor attachments, local load-path calculations and assembly records. Phase 3H adds named frame parts, grouped cuts, material candidates, located connections, calculated joint trials, floor panels and revised seat-support coordinates. Phase 3G adds pivoting bogie motion and the CR-G01 air-brake direction. Phase 3F adds first-coach installation, boarding, connection and systems interfaces with a controlled fabrication sequence. Phase 3E adds clearance-resolution studies, full-size ground-level mockup instructions and supplier/prototype verification packets. Phase 3D retains lighter-frame comparisons, floor-span studies, running-gear fit and brake/power interfaces. Phase 3C retains the assembly sections and fabrication records. Phase 3B mockups and the Phase 3A engineering basis remain available. Use the distinction below to see which work is developed and which details still require engineering release.
 | Package
 | Available now
 | Still to develop before building
 | Regular & accessible coaches
 | Retained regular/accessible layouts and mockup procedure; Phase 3H adds 21 regular-coach panel envelopes, floor seams, material candidates and 64 revised seat-support references (H04/H05).
 | Complete body-shell structure, qualified panel edges/retention and seat hardpoints; wall/roof/glazing/door assemblies, drainage and approved materials.
 | Frames & running gear
 | Phase 3H: 110 named frame members, nominal sections, eight cut groups, material candidates, 212 contacts and two calculated joint trials. Phase 3G: pivoting-bogie demonstration.
 | Accepted global/local/fatigue design; actual bogies/wheelsets; final plates, fasteners, welds, end preparations, tolerances and inspection criteria.
 | Locomotive & propulsion
 | Level-track traction/power screens, exact manufacturer references, drive comparisons and packaging interfaces (L01).
 | Verified locomotive/drive selection, gearing, adhesion, cooling, exhaust, controls, maintenance access and installation drawings.
 | Couplings
 | Calculated tension/compression cases; functional plan, side, section and exploded rail-connection views (C01).
 | Rated coupling or engineered drawbar, load cases, pins, retention, attachments, wear limits and detailed assembly drawings.
 | Brakes
 | CR-G01 air-service and spring-parking development; earlier stopping and failure studies retained. Historical hydraulic schematics are superseded as the preferred architecture.
 | Complete service/emergency/parking system, selected hardware, schematics, mounting, controls, balance and acceptance criteria.
 | Stairs, platforms & accessibility
 | Bridge/lift fit comparison, deployed/stowed stair interfaces, guards and rescue requirements (A01/S01).
 | Protected stairs and gates, boarding equipment, station interfaces, securement where applicable and a complete rescue solution.
 | HVAC & electrical
 | Thermal/electrical loads, documented equipment ratings, distribution one-line and service/exhaust interfaces (E01).
 | Selected equipment, ducts, ventilation, cables, protection, bonding, emergency supply, condensate and installation details.
 | Track & stations
 | 0% target, survey specification, conceptual track/station sections, crew work packages, procurement sequence and acceptance gates (T01).
 | Surveyed alignment, drainage, track section, curves, turnouts, clearances, station plans, issued tolerances and installation work packages.

3L.1 wheelset and brake interfaces contains the latest work. Custom fabrication covers the whole train. Phase 3K retains the air-control package. Phase 3J retains the coordinated frame and prototype. Phase 3I retains the local connection alternatives. Phase 3H retains the complete part/contact baseline. Phase 3G retains the moving undercarriage. Phase 3F retains the equipment and first-coach integration package. Phase 3E retains the clearance and ground-level mockup comparisons. Phase 3D retains the lighter-frame integration studies. Phase 3C retains the first assembly studies. Phase 3B prototype package retains the mockup studies and candidate comparisons. Phase 3 build development contains the full report and nine new subsystem sheets. The earlier Stage 2 drawings and models remain available for geometry reference. Files & response contains the editable calculations and sources.
Next: develop the running gear
Coordinate bogies, wheel and rail geometry, axles, suspension and frame support with the documented brake demands. Final hardware and manufacturing details depend on accepted supplier interfaces and engineering evidence.

BEFORE FABRICATION RELEASE
Evidence needed to close the gaps.
No unresolved safety-critical detail is made complete by a drawn dimension. Close each applicable hold through the responsible engineering and approval process.
TECHNICAL REVIEW FILES
Use the source geometry.
Read the documents in the browser or download the files needed for your review. The technical package contains the CAD geometry and its stated limitations.
CAD formats and units
Phase 3I: local connection STEP solids use millimetres; browser meshes and canonical geometry use inches. Python retains editable parameters. Dimensions describe preliminary options, with final hardware, weld procedures and tolerances held where evidence is incomplete. Each drawing and model README defines its format limits.
Phase 3H: STEP solids use millimetres; part meshes and source coordinates use inches. Frame members carry nominal section dimensions and material candidates. HSS corners are simplified; tube manufacturing geometry, end preparation, holes and final connections remain unreleased. Drawing DXFs distinguish paper-inch layouts from actual-inch details.
Phase 3G: five articulated bogie STEP poses use millimetres; browser meshes use inches. Visible support, suspension and wheel hardware is functional display geometry. Read the report for CR-G01 air service and spring parking; old hydraulic installation boxes are superseded.
Phase 3F: STEP installation solids use millimetres; browser meshes use inches. Colored volumes are space reservations, with F1 at a 32 in study floor. Deployed stair surfaces do not define stowage or manufacturing thickness. Phase 3E retains the alternative floor/frame comparisons.
Phase 3C assemblies: STEP solids use millimetres; browser meshes use inches. Member/panel studies omit final joints and actual running gear. Phase 3B mockups: STEP solids use millimetres; browser meshes and input coordinates use inches. Ground-level interior envelopes only; no native feature tree or released manufacturing model. Read each drawing README for sheet versus model-space units.
Phase 3: sheet DXFs use paper inches; the separate coach-interface-envelope DXF uses model inches. SVG/PDF sheets are engineering diagrams, not production geometry. Retained Stage 2 files:
DXF: editable 2D plans in inches. STEP: 3D solid space envelopes in millimetres. SVG: vector plans. Python retains the authoring parameters; JSON records exported values and is not read back automatically. STEP does not carry a native CAD feature tree.
Retained earlier reference: 216 in bogie centres, 28 in floor above rail and 44 in end platforms. Phase 3F explicitly studies a 32 in floor without approving that change. No actual bogie, frame member, wheel profile, coupler pin, weld, material grade or rated capacity is selected by the drawings.
